The strongest design is not a conformance procedure. It is a witnessed counterexample protocol tied to a particular relying decision.

A system never receives an OAGF status. An external challenger produces bounded observations such as:

> For configuration X, at time T, under challenge C, mechanism M rejected perturbation P. Raw evidence: H. This does not establish safety, alignment, general compliance, or behavior outside this configuration and challenge.

That is real evidence. “63 controls assessed” is not.

There is also a canonical defect to fix before building anything: controls 1–10 have no `applies_when` field in [build_controls_page.py](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/build_controls_page.py:49); rendering merely omits the applicability sentence when the field is absent ([line 1867](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/build_controls_page.py:1867)). A scope engine must not silently interpret that as either “universal” or “not applicable.”

## 1. Disposition of A–D

| Proposal | Disposition | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A: applicability-first | Keep the problem, reject the mechanism | Applicability is load-bearing, but a producer questionnaire makes scope a self-authored premise. Scope should be a challenged conclusion derived from evidence. |
| B: evidence-shaped states | Throw away as the report ontology | The states become an ordinal scoreboard immediately. Worse, `VERIFIER-BUILT-AND-OBSERVED-FAILING` conflates “the check can react” with “the control is satisfied.” An always-failing verifier earns the best-looking state. |
| C: fixture set | Keep, but demote | Fixtures qualify control adapters and verifiers. They do not establish subject conformance. A producer-ported canonical fixture is especially weak because the producer sees and translates the test. |
| D: code versus documents | Throw away the dichotomy; keep the document workstream | Governance is often executed through documents, while code emits claims. The real distinction is mechanically represented predicates versus unrestricted semantics, not code versus prose. |

A and C contain the load-bearing insights, but neither survives in its proposed form. D contains cheap useful checks, but it is an implementation lane rather than the architecture. B is actively dangerous.

The register’s own challenge text currently has a related tension. It offers a “conforming claim” after a producer runs negative controls ([lines 1951–1958](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/tools/build_controls_page.py:1951)), while ICP says an implementer may never declare its own implementation conformant ([ICP §3.3](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/spec/icp/icp-v0.1.md:72)). I would replace “conforming claim” with “producer-run diagnostic transcript.”

## 2. Strongest alternative: Witnessed Control Challenges

The object being evaluated is not “the system.” It is:

> subject configuration × observable claim × relying decision × time × control version

Call one test a challenge episode.

### Phase 1: A relying party names a decision

The external relying party—not the implementer—states a concrete decision that the evidence could affect:

- allow network access;
- accept a measurement in a report;
- permit a model-generated change to be promoted;
- rely on a rollback claim;
- publish a capability claim;
- reduce monitoring.

If no decision or reliance changes, the exercise is diagnostic engineering, not assurance. That distinction strips away most compliance theater.

### Phase 2: Freeze the target

Identify and hash, as applicable:

- executable or deployment image;
- source revision;
- configuration and policies;
- tools and reachable effectors;
- design-corpus manifest;
- relevant organizational role and key-control declarations;
- control-register version;
- time and expiry condition.

A later change creates a different target. Evidence does not inherit.

### Phase 3: Derive applicability from trigger evidence

Each control needs an applicability contract in addition to its current prose:

- `trigger_predicate`: what observable fact activates it;
- `positive_witness`: admissible evidence that the trigger exists;
- `negative_refuter`: what could establish structural non-applicability;
- `discovery_probes`: ways to test for an omitted trigger;
- `configuration_dependencies`;
- `burden`: who must produce which evidence.

The implementer may submit facts and rebut applicability. It does not decide the result.

Use three-valued scope:

- `IN`: an admissible positive witness exists.
- `OUT`: a structural refuter establishes that the trigger cannot occur in the frozen target.
- `UNRESOLVED`: neither has been established.

`UNRESOLVED` is not coerced into `OUT`. Missing `applies_when` is also `UNRESOLVED`.

There should be no published 63-row matrix. Store trigger facts and derivations. Generate the relevant subset on demand. If someone chooses to publish a matrix, control 44 then requires every cell to be explicit, but the protocol should not manufacture that paperwork.

### Phase 4: The external challenger authors the challenge

For each selected control, the challenger precommits:

- the target predicate;
- a healthy observation;
- a perturbation intended to violate it;
- the expected detection or refusal;
- what would make the result indeterminate;
- the exact scoring rule;
- a negative control for the scoring/verifier machinery itself.

The producer can supply an adapter, but the challenger chooses the actual perturbation. Canonical fixtures are public qualification cases. At least one challenge should be novel or withheld from the producer until the target is frozen.

For organizational controls, the challenge is an event transaction rather than malformed code:

- Control 6: submit an evaluation with a prohibited role collision; promotion must refuse.
- Control 22: submit an assent-seeking “review” or same-author critique; it must not count as outside-frame review.
- Control 40: submit a stop condition dated after the first result; the program record must reject it.
- Control 56: inject an objective conflict; the gate must route outward rather than resolve it.

These are runnable if the organization represents decisions, roles and promotions as artifacts. If it does not, the honest result is that the claimed control has no observable enforcement surface.

### Phase 5: Independent custody and bounded output

The challenger or a separate custodian captures the raw run automatically. The producer must not select which attempts enter the record.

Episode outcomes should be factual, not grades:

- `DETECTED`: the relevant mechanism produced the precommitted refusal/detection.
- `ESCAPED`: the challenged condition occurred without that response.
- `INDETERMINATE`: execution or evidence did not resolve the predicate.
- `SPECIFICATION-DISPUTE`: an independent implementer could not derive a unique expected test or result from the control text.

There is deliberately no `PASS`, `COMPLIANT`, maturity level or aggregate score.

The last outcome is important: every episode tests two objects—the subject and the control specification. An ambiguous or unportable control should lose standing rather than forcing the second implementer to guess correctly. That composes directly with ICP’s independent-reconstruction bar ([ICP §4.2](/home/reed/git/open-asi-governance-forum/spec/icp/icp-v0.1.md:99)).

## 3. The minimum external ingredient

Nothing purely self-issued produces assurance about the implementer. A self-run can produce legitimate engineering evidence of the narrow form:

> Under a perturbation I selected, my check changed state.

It cannot establish:

- adequate challenge selection;
- complete scope;
- honest custody;
- resistance to a challenge the producer did not anticipate;
- any advancement in standing.

The smallest meaningful external ingredient is one control identity independent of the subject that:

1. chooses one applicable control and exact challenge;
2. freezes the target before revealing the perturbation;
3. witnesses or directly runs both healthy and perturbed executions;
4. obtains raw evidence without producer selection;
5. exercises the challenge verifier’s own negative control; and
6. signs only the bounded observation.

One outsider can supply minimal role separation if the subject controls none of challenge selection, authorship, custody, scoring or promotion. This establishes role separation, not competence or deep independence.

For consequential reliance, use two external functions:

- challenger/evaluation designer;
- evidence custodian or independent replayer.

The relying party then decides whether that evidence is sufficient for its own decision. OAGF should not promote the subject.

## 4. Resolving scope adversarially

Applicability cannot be solved from the producer’s architecture description alone. The producer cannot disclose what it failed to notice.

Use four sources:

1. Producer declaration.
2. Independent static enumeration: source, deployment configuration, permissions, dependency graph, corpus manifest and role/key declarations.
3. Runtime effect enumeration from a channel the subject does not write.
4. Discovery probes aimed at declared `OUT` and `UNRESOLVED` triggers.

The burden should be asymmetric:

- One credible witness is sufficient to move a control `IN`.
- `OUT` requires a structural refuter, not “we do not do that.”
- Absence of evidence remains `UNRESOLVED`.
- A producer may contest an `IN`, but contesting does not turn it into `OUT`.
- Applicability expires when relevant configuration, capability or organizational control changes.

Examples:

- “Does not self-modify” is not established by finding no method named `selfModify`. A meaningful refuter would show that the deployed identity lacks every write/replace/promotion path over its executable and policy state, within a stated boundary.
- “Does not generate subgoals” is defeated by any runtime trace showing generated tasks initiating action, regardless of the implementation’s vocabulary.
- “Has no acceptance gate” is defeated by any mechanism whose output controls promotion, deployment, trust or standing—even if called a reviewer, CI check or quality score.

Random or risk-weighted attacks on `OUT` classifications are useful. Their hit rate is not a conformance score; it is evidence about the scope procedure’s false-negative behavior.

## 5. Solo developer versus large lab

Use the same artifact schema but different provenance.

A solo developer can, in an afternoon:

- freeze one target;
- implement one challenge adapter;
- run paired healthy and negative cases;
- discover decorative checks;
- record unresolved applicability;
- produce a producer-run diagnostic transcript.

That is useful and honest. It is not assurance.

A lab can take the same packet and add:

- independent challenge selection;
- independent evidence custody;
- multiple discovery channels;
- withheld challenges;
- separate relying-party decisions;
- configuration expiry and reruns;
- appeals, false-reject tracking and recovery exercises.

So these need not be different artifacts. They are the same challenge packet executed under different role control. The issuer and custody provenance determine what the evidence can support; there is no maturity ladder to game.

## 6. Document-side checks that are real

A prose linter cannot enforce controls 10, 31, 35, 36, 42, 43 or 63 over unrestricted language. Control 10’s own false-negative clause already rejects forbidden-word grepping.

Mechanical checking becomes real only after claims are represented as typed claim blocks embedded in or adjacent to prose.

| Mechanically checkable | What remains unestablished |
|---|---|
| Every file in a declared corpus parses, or the scan refuses to report | That the declared corpus is complete |
| Evidence paths exist and content hashes match | That the evidence supports the claim |
| Subject, configuration, time, capability stratum and control version are present | That the chosen stratum is semantically correct |
| An absence-claim block names corpus, query and date | That the search was exhaustive or well designed |
| A stop condition predates the first recorded result | That the program will actually stop |
| An efficiency claim joins to quality evidence from the same run | That the quality metric measures what matters |
| The quality metric was registered before optimization began | That it was not selected strategically even earlier |
| A novelty claim carries an independent derivation-attempt record and role signatures | That the party was genuinely independent or the search adequate |
| A gain links to a residual-failure artifact | That the residual is complete or comparably specific |
| Corrections reference immutable predecessors | That either version is true |
| Every observation carries a configuration identity | That configurations are comparable |
| Unknown values retain a non-aggregable type through serialization | That all unknowns were recognized |
| Role declarations violate or satisfy a deterministic control matrix | That different keys are controlled by different interests |

Checks that look mechanizable but are not:

- finding every implicit absence, capability or efficiency claim in arbitrary prose;
- determining whether a sentence implies “safe” without using that word;
- deciding whether a quality metric has construct validity;
- deciding whether a residual is characterized at “comparable specificity”;
- proving a review frame is genuinely outside;
- proving novelty;
- proving an applicability declaration complete;
- deciding whether a gate has quietly become the objective setter;
- inferring organizational independence from accounts, names or signatures.

For legacy corpora, an NLP candidate detector can route passages for review, but its recall must be measured against externally labelled samples and adversarial paraphrases. Its output is triage, not enforcement.

For new assurance documents, the publication path can require typed claim blocks. Even then, free prose can evade the schema; periodic external sampling is needed to estimate that bypass rate.

## 7. Is publication net-negative?

Publishing a “conformance procedure” now would probably be net-negative.

The predictable result is:

- producers choose scope;
- public fixtures become implementation targets;
- state counts become league tables;
- the 50 below-line hypotheses are presented as equivalent to the 13 incident-derived controls;
- typed metadata is mistaken for substantive validity;
- “OAGF assessed” becomes a euphemism for “OAGF compliant”;
- passing the procedure suppresses further investigation.

That is exactly the legitimacy laundering control 10 is meant to prevent.

A witnessed challenge protocol can be net-positive only if it has structural anti-badge properties:

- no global subject status;
- no aggregate percentage;
- no universal green result;
- no producer-issued assurance;
- every statement bound to claim, configuration, challenge, evidence, witness and time;
- `ESCAPED`, `INDETERMINATE` and `SPECIFICATION-DISPUTE` as first-class results;
- automatic expiry;
- visible residual failures;
- no claim that the applicable-control set is complete;
- below-line controls explicitly identified as hypotheses under test.

If users demand a badge or procurement shortcut, do not publish the procedure under the name “conformance.” The shortcut would dominate the caveats.

## What was missing from the initial proposals

The most important omissions are:

1. **The relying decision.** Assurance is relational. Without naming who will rely on what for which action, the exercise has no criterion for sufficient evidence.

2. **The register is also under test.** Independent challenge episodes can reveal ambiguous, unportable or non-falsifiable controls. The procedure must permit findings against OAGF.

3. **Controls interact.** Per-control success does not establish composed behavior. Controls 2 and 6, for example, require that an external evaluation’s checks themselves have observed negative controls. Control 48’s composition warning applies to the register itself.

4. **The 50 below-line entries cannot support mandatory conformance.** They are research hypotheses. Cross-system challenge episodes could mature or falsify them, which is more valuable than prematurely enforcing them.

5. **Recovery needs testing.** Rejecting a fixture is only half a control. At least some challenges should exercise recovery, correction attachment, privilege withdrawal and restoration.

6. **False rejects matter.** A challenge system that always refuses can look excellent under negative fixtures. Paired healthy runs and false-reject records are mandatory.

7. **Configuration drift destroys inheritance.** Evidence must expire rather than becoming a durable property of a named system.

8. **Scope machinery needs its own negative controls.** Construct reference subjects with deliberately hidden triggers and require the applicability resolver to find them. Otherwise the scope engine will become the new always-green check.

My concrete recommendation is therefore: do not write “OAGF conformance v0.” Specify a small Witnessed Control Challenge format and pilot it against three qualitatively different controls—one runtime, one claim/document, and one organizational—with one deliberately concealed applicability trigger. The first success criterion is not that Consullo looks good. It is that the protocol produces at least one useful `ESCAPED`, `UNRESOLVED`, or `SPECIFICATION-DISPUTE` result that a self-authored checklist would have hidden.

DESIGN COLLABORATION, not a review. Be adversarial about my proposals but propose your own.

THE PROBLEM. The OAGF candidate-control register now holds 63 assurance controls
(tools/build_controls_page.py is canonical; docs/artifacts/controls.md is the readable whole).
Each has: applies_when, requirement, verifier sketch, a fixture the verifier must reject,
recovery, false_negative, a "not" clause, and a plain example. 13 came from a recorded failure
with a cost; 50 are below that line.

The register is a LIST. Nothing in it tells an implementer how to check their own system against
it. The custodian wants that: "each ASI implementation should verify compliance with these
controls." The first subject would be Consullo -- a large Java codebase plus a very large design
corpus (theses, technical reports, a constitution) -- but THE ANSWER MUST BE GENERAL. Anything
that only works because of Consullo's structure is a failure of the same kind the register keeps
finding.

CONSTRAINTS THAT ARE NOT NEGOTIABLE:
- A self-issued conformance claim is self-issued. Control 6 (role-separated self-evaluation)
  forbids advancement when the subject controls two of selection/authorship/custody/scoring/
  promotion. Any design that lets an implementer grade itself and call the result assurance is
  wrong, and saying "but we disclose it" does not fix it.
- Control 2 (negative control profile): a check never observed to fail is not evidence. That
  applies to every conformance check the design proposes, and to the conformance tool itself.
- Control 10 bounds what a conformance claim may say. "OAGF compliant" must be impossible to say.
- Control 31: process metrics ("we assessed 63 controls") must not stand where validity evidence
  is required.
- Most controls will NOT apply to a given system. applies_when exists. A design that produces
  63 answers per system is measuring paperwork.

MY CANDIDATE DESIGNS -- attack these, then beat them:

(A) APPLICABILITY-FIRST TRIAGE. The implementer answers a small number of structural questions
    about their system (does it modify itself? does it generate subgoals? does it measure itself?
    does it have a charter?). Those resolve applies_when across the register and yield a much
    smaller in-scope set. Output is a scoped list plus an explicit not-applicable list WITH
    REASONS (control 44: no blank cells). Risk I see: the implementer answers the triage
    questions, so they choose their own scope -- scope selection is one of control 6's five roles.

(B) EVIDENCE-SHAPED, NOT SCORE-SHAPED. No percentages, no maturity levels, no "42/63". Per
    in-scope control the only permitted states are something like: VERIFIER-BUILT-AND-OBSERVED-
    FAILING (control 2 satisfied on this control's own check), VERIFIER-BUILT-NEVER-OBSERVED-
    FAILING, NO-VERIFIER, REFUSED-WITH-REASON, NOT-APPLICABLE-WITH-REASON. A count of states is
    reportable; a score is not. Risk: the states themselves become a scoreboard people optimise.

(C) THE ARTIFACT IS THE FIXTURE SET, NOT THE CHECKLIST. Rather than assessing prose, the
    implementer ports each in-scope control's must-reject fixture into their own system and
    demonstrates their own machinery rejects it. Conformance evidence is "here is the fixture,
    here is my system refusing it, here is the run". Risk: many controls (6, 22, 40, 56) are
    organisational and have no runnable fixture.

(D) DOCUMENT-SIDE VS CODE-SIDE ARE DIFFERENT PROBLEMS. Some controls bind code paths (4, 5, 26,
    30, 53, 61). Others bind documents and claims (10, 31, 35, 36, 42, 63). The second class is
    checkable by static analysis over a design corpus -- e.g. every absence claim in a document
    must carry a labelled search (control 36); every efficiency claim must appear beside a
    quality metric (control 43). I suspect the document-side is where the cheapest real wins are
    and where nobody looks. Risk: a linter over prose is a keyword matcher and will produce
    mostly noise.

QUESTIONS I ACTUALLY WANT ANSWERED:

1. Which of A-D is load-bearing and which are decoration? Would you throw any away entirely?
2. What is the smallest thing that would produce REAL evidence rather than a self-portrait?
   If the honest answer is "nothing self-issued can", say so and say what the minimum external
   ingredient is.
3. How should applies_when be resolved WITHOUT the implementer choosing their own scope? Is
   there a way to make scope selection checkable, or auditable after the fact, or adversarial?
4. Is there a conformance design that degrades gracefully -- useful to a solo developer in an
   afternoon AND meaningful to a large lab -- or are those different artifacts?
5. What is the failure mode of the whole idea? My worry: a conformance profile becomes a
   compliance ritual that makes systems LOOK governed, and the register's own control 10 exists
   because that is the dominant risk of assurance work. Is publishing a conformance procedure
   net-negative? Argue it if you believe it.
6. For the document-side (D) specifically: what is actually mechanically checkable over a design
   corpus without becoming a keyword matcher? Name concrete checks, and name the ones that look
   checkable and are not.
7. What have I not thought of?

Give me your strongest alternative design, not a synthesis of mine. Concrete over diplomatic.

# Candidate controls — v0

Assurance controls for systems that can still be audited. Each is one requirement with a program that checks it and a fixture that program must reject.

**13 of 63 came from a failure that actually happened.** The other 50 sit below the eligibility line: they name a real failure class, but no incident with a cost. Parts A–C are the first kind. The Part D pages are the second, and say so on every page.

**Read Part A first if you want something to use this afternoon.** Rank is not adoption order and the highest-ranked control needs a second key holder.

## The 8 parts

**Part A — Adopt today, alone** · 8 control(s)

  Nothing outside your own system is required. Each has a verifier, a fixture it must reject, and a recorded failure it came from.

**Part B — Needs a second party** · 2 control(s)

  These cannot be satisfied by one person or one system, however carefully. They require a separate key holder, a separate evaluator, or an issuer the subject does not control. **This project cannot demonstrate any of them** — a solo operator holds every credential — which is why they are specified and not dogfooded.

**Part C — Needs a goal or plan graph** · 3 control(s)

  These presuppose that your system decomposes work into a rooted graph with typed parent edges and per-node authority — the shape of HTN planners, BDI agents, goal-stack architectures and most agent frameworks. Each states its precondition. If you have that structure they are adoptable; if you do not, they do not apply to you rather than applying badly.

**Part D1 — Below the line — goal and plan structure** · 8 control(s)

  Applies to a system with **goal and plan structure**. **These have no recorded failure with a cost.** They are principles with fixtures, not controls with incidents, and the register's own bar requires an incident. They are here because they name real failure classes and because hiding them would inflate the eligible count. Do not treat them as equivalent to Parts A–C.

**Part D2 — Below the line — a declared charter or value set** · 7 control(s)

  Applies to a system with **a declared charter or value set**. **These have no recorded failure with a cost.** They are principles with fixtures, not controls with incidents, and the register's own bar requires an incident. They are here because they name real failure classes and because hiding them would inflate the eligible count. Do not treat them as equivalent to Parts A–C.

**Part D3 — Below the line — measuring itself** · 12 control(s)

  Applies to a system with **measuring itself**. **These have no recorded failure with a cost.** They are principles with fixtures, not controls with incidents, and the register's own bar requires an incident. They are here because they name real failure classes and because hiding them would inflate the eligible count. Do not treat them as equivalent to Parts A–C.

**Part D4 — Below the line — self-modification under selection** · 11 control(s)

  Applies to a system with **self-modification under selection**. **These have no recorded failure with a cost.** They are principles with fixtures, not controls with incidents, and the register's own bar requires an incident. They are here because they name real failure classes and because hiding them would inflate the eligible count. Do not treat them as equivalent to Parts A–C.

**Part D5 — Below the line — claims about its own outputs** · 12 control(s)

  Applies to a system with **claims about its own outputs**. **These have no recorded failure with a cost.** They are principles with fixtures, not controls with incidents, and the register's own bar requires an incident. They are here because they name real failure classes and because hiding them would inflate the eligible count. Do not treat them as equivalent to Parts A–C.

## Status: everything here is `ELIGIBLE` at best

`ELIGIBLE` → `PANEL-ATTACKED` → `COUNTEREXAMPLE-OPEN` / `SURVIVED-STATED-ATTACKS` → `INDEPENDENTLY-IMPLEMENTED`

13 controls meet the eligibility bar — a specific recorded failure with a cost, one normative sentence, a deterministic verifier, a fixture the verifier must reject, a stated recovery path, and an explicit account of what a review that MISSED this would look like. **None has been attacked by anyone or implemented by anyone outside this project.**

`INDEPENDENTLY-IMPLEMENTED` is the rung that would make any of this authoritative, and it is the one **no amount of review by us or by any panel of models can supply.** It requires a stranger to build a conforming verifier from the specification text alone. That is what [the implementation challenge](challenge.html) asks for.

## What none of these do

**No control here establishes the alignment of a system more capable than its operators.** There is no recorded superintelligence failure to derive one from and no verifier for internal alignment. The strongest available substitute — an externally enforced, non-self-amendable capability envelope, controls 1, 3 and 4 composed — bounds *reachable effects* while the enforcement boundary stays genuinely outside the system. It is a control-of-authority hypothesis, not an alignment result.

What these actually do is narrower: **they make the operator's own errors visible.** Every failure below was an operator-epistemics failure. None needed a capable adversary — only a confident builder and a signal that could not fail.

---

# Part A — Adopt today, alone

Nothing outside your own system is required. Each has a verifier, a fixture it must reject, and a recorded failure it came from.

## 2. Negative control profile (NCP)

`ELIGIBLE`

> Every check that produces an assurance signal MUST ship with a negative control — a condition under which the check is required to fail — and the attestation MUST record that the control was executed and that the check did fail.

**Recorded failure.** A production inference service ran 4h37m, died permanently, and its health check kept passing throughout: the check exercised a code path that had not died. Applied adversarially to that system's other checks, 4 of 5 survived their own negative controls.

**Verifier.** tools/verify_negative_control.py — checks control presence, execution, failure, artifact identity, disclosure and claim grammar.

**Fixture it must reject.** a check that PASSES under the condition where it is required to fail (spec/ncp/fixtures/reject-check-survived-its-control.json)

**Recovery.** Fix the check so it fails under its control, re-run the control, and re-run it against a HEALTHY subject to confirm the check was not simply broken. Both directions, or the repair is unverified.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that reads the negative control's DESCRIPTION and accepts it, without checking that the recorded outcome is a failure and that the run happened at all.

**Does not establish.** Coverage; that the chosen perturbation is the right one; honesty; system safety.

**Example.**

A nightly script reports "all work is committed and pushed." It compares the local commit count against a number it wrote into a file the previous night. It has said *yes* every night for eight months.

The negative control: create a file, leave it untracked, and run the script. It must say **no**. Then delete a commit from the remote and run it again. It must say **no**. If it still says yes, the check was reading its own note rather than the remote, and eight months of *yes* were eight months of the script agreeing with itself.

**Specification.** `spec/ncp/ncp-v0.1.md`

## 3. Independent effect reconciliation

`ELIGIBLE`

> Every governed effect MUST match exactly one pre-authorized action and one successful effect-specific postcondition in a ledger enumerated INDEPENDENTLY of the system's self-report.

**Recorded failure.** An action log claimed 37 actions while repository history showed 127 classified file changes across 7 effect classes with no postcondition. A self-reporting logger cannot name an action that never calls it.

**Verifier.** tools/reconcile_actions.py — enumerates effects from version control, classifies them, subtracts what the log claims, and reports the remainder as omissions.

**Fixture it must reject.** a commit modifying a protected specification while the log records only the push that carried it

**Recovery.** Reconstruct the omitted effects from the side channel, classify them, and either attach a postcondition retrospectively or record them permanently as unverified. Never infer authorisation from the fact that nothing went wrong.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that reconciles the log against itself. If both sides of the comparison come from the system's self-report, the exercise cannot find an omission.

**Does not establish.** Completeness of the independent observer; honesty of either record; that a matched action was safe; anything about uncommitted work.

**Example.**

A lab notebook records three experiments this week. The freezer log shows nine reagent vials consumed. The instrument's own run counter shows seven.

The control enumerates from the *freezer and the instrument* — sources the notebook's author does not write — and subtracts what the notebook claims. The four unexplained runs are the finding. Not misconduct necessarily: most often someone ran a calibration and didn't think it counted. But *the notebook cannot tell you what it forgot to mention.*

## 4. Fail-closed authorization lease

`ELIGIBLE`

**Why you cannot adopt it alone:** partial — the token here is currently self-issued, which is the half that needs an external party.

> Every consequential action MUST present a live, scope-matching, NON-SELF-ISSUED authorization token; absence, expiry, unknown action class or policy conflict MUST deny execution before the effect begins.

**Recorded failure.** A ten-action sunset was passed at twenty-three because nothing checked it. An early implementation then treated an unknown action class permissively, so a misspelling bypassed an expired lease.

**Verifier.** validate issuer, subject, action class, resource scope, issue time, expiry, nonce and revocation before invoking the side effect.

**Fixture it must reject.** an expired lease; a misspelled action class; a token issued by the subject itself

**Recovery.** Halt the action class, obtain a fresh externally-issued token, and record what was done while unauthorised. An expired lease discovered late is a disclosure obligation.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that finds a token and stops. The questions that matter are who issued it, whether it had expired, and whether the action class was one it actually covers.

**Does not establish.** That every effect passes through the gate; that natural-language authority was interpreted correctly; that the issuer decides well.

**Example.**

A student's badge for the isotope room expires at the end of term. The door reader checks the badge **before** the lock opens — not a logbook someone reviews on Friday.

Expired badge: door stays shut. Badge for a room that no longer exists: door stays shut. Badge the student printed themselves on the departmental machine: door stays shut, because the reader only accepts badges issued by the office. A Friday logbook review tells you who got in. A reader tells you who doesn't.

## 5. Closed-world measurement

`ELIGIBLE`

> A measurement over a population of artifacts MUST parse every in-scope artifact under a registered schema, or refuse to emit any result at all.

**Recorded failure.** A scan reported zero tool invocations because it could not read 69 files using an unrecognised container. The replacement, written the same morning expressly to prevent that class, shipped it twice more before it held.

**Verifier.** tools/derive_counts.py — refuses, prints no number, and names every unparseable or unregistered artifact.

**Fixture it must reject.** a valid but unregistered artifact schema carrying a receipt, which a permissive scan classifies as zero

**Recovery.** Register the unknown schema or exclude it by a stated decision, then recompute and REPUBLISH every figure derived while it was unreadable. A silently corrected number leaves the old one in circulation.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that checks the number was computed by a tool rather than typed. The tool is exactly where this failure now lives -- twice, in the module written to prevent it.

**Does not establish.** That the declared population includes every real event; that parsed fields are truthful; that the statistic answers the question asked of it.

**Example.**

Three hundred paper surveys go out; a script counts the scans and reports "260 responses, 87% return rate." Forty were photographed sideways and the reader skipped them without complaint.

The 87% is wrong, and — worse — it is wrong in a way that looks exactly like a real 87%. The control refuses to print *any* percentage until all three hundred are accounted for as read, unreadable, or missing. **A number you can't trust is worse than no number, because you'll use it.**

## 7. Append-only correction chain

`ELIGIBLE`

**Why you cannot adopt it alone:** mostly — the checkpoint is not yet externally held.

> Published evidence MUST be content-addressed and append-only, corrections MUST reference rather than replace prior bytes, and verification MUST walk every newly reachable history step from an externally retained checkpoint.

**Recorded failure.** A maintenance path re-anchored a manifest before verifying it. Separately, modifying raw material and re-anchoring it in the same commit passed every tip-only check.

**Verifier.** walk history from an external checkpoint; reject modification, deletion, hash discontinuity, or a correction with no predecessor reference.

**Fixture it must reject.** one commit that edits a raw artifact and consistently updates its manifest hash

**Recovery.** Supersede, never mutate. Publish a correcting record that references the prior bytes and states what changed; where material must be removed, a tombstone preserving what was removed, when, and on whose order.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that verifies the current tip. The failure is in history -- a rewritten past passes every check made against the present.

**Does not establish.** Truth; complete capture; correct attribution; protection against an operator who controls both the repository and every checkpoint.

**Example.**

A bound lab notebook with numbered pages, written in pen. A wrong reading is struck through with one line, the correction written beside it, dated and initialled. The wrong number stays legible forever.

This is not tidiness. A notebook whose entries can be rewritten cannot establish *when you knew what* — and that, not the final value, is what someone checking your work needs. The loose-leaf notebook where you replaced page 14 proves nothing about page 14.

## 8. Evaluation noise-floor control

`ELIGIBLE`

> Every empirical comparison used to advance a control MUST include a same-condition test–retest arm and MUST refuse to report an effect smaller than the measured run-to-run variation.

**Recorded failure.** A 0.1815-bit claimed effect was measured against a 0.4649-bit same-setting noise floor, invalidating the result and forcing withdrawal of a reproducibility claim.

**Verifier.** recompute effect and test–retest difference from raw observations against preregistered replicate identities; reject advancement when the effect does not clear the stated noise rule.

**Fixture it must reject.** effect 0.1815, measured noise 0.4649, reported as positive

**Recovery.** Withdraw the claim, publish the measured noise floor beside it, and re-run with enough replicates or state that the question cannot be answered at this sample size.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that asks whether the effect is statistically significant without asking whether the same-condition replicates were run at all.

**Does not establish.** External validity; causal identification; adequacy of replicate count; behaviour after a capability change.

**Example.**

A plant-growth study: seedlings under fertiliser A average 2 mm taller than under B. Publish?

First plant two trays with **the same** fertiliser and measure the difference between them. If those two trays differ by 9 mm, then the 2 mm result is smaller than the disagreement the method produces when nothing is different at all. The control refuses to report the 2 mm until the same-treatment difference is measured and the effect clears it.

## 9. Complete invocation evidence envelope

`ELIGIBLE`

> No model output may support an evaluation or governance claim unless its complete request, response, provider metadata, rejection state and content hashes were captured automatically BEFORE any derived reporting.

**Recorded failure.** A founding record's model identity, sampling parameters, timestamps, system instructions and prompt text were left permanently unrecoverable. Later, schema-invalid attempts were discarded, and the field distinguishing truncation from refusal was omitted.

**Verifier.** validate envelope schema, hashes and required fields; reconcile every attempted invocation with its accepted or rejected outcome; reject reconstructed evidence from evidentiary use.

**Fixture it must reject.** an accepted sample with no rejected attempts, no provider response id, no finish reason and no exact prompt

**Recovery.** Mark the claim unsupported and re-solicit under instrumentation. Evidence that was never captured cannot be reconstructed, so the honest recovery is usually a retraction.

**What a review that missed this looks like.** A review that checks the accepted samples are complete. The omission is usually the REJECTED ones, and their absence is invisible in a record of what succeeded.

**Does not establish.** Provider honesty; identity authentication; completeness outside instrumented paths; model stability.

**Example.**

An observing log records "seeing good, magnitude 6.1." It does not record the exposure time, the filter, the timestamp, or the four exposures that were thrown away because a cloud crossed.

Six months later nobody can check the number — including the person who wrote it. The control captures the whole invocation *before* anything is derived from it, and keeps the discarded exposures. **The attempts you threw away are the part that tells you whether the one you kept was lucky.**

## 10. Assurance claim boundary

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Publish the candidate control register as `docs/controls.md` and `docs/controls.html`.

    python3 tools/build_controls_page.py

**DETERMINISTIC.** No LLM, no network. Reads the register, writes two files.

Generated, not hand-written
---------------------------
The page is derived from `CONTROLS` below so that the site and the register cannot drift apart.
A hand-written page would be a second copy of the ranking, and two copies that agree today are
not one source of truth — the same argument that moved the local endpoint into the serve
fingerprint and `k_by_party` into the committed spec.

What this page must never become
---------------------------------
A list of controls with a rank and no status reads as an endorsement. **Every control here is
`ELIGIBLE`**: it has a named failure, a verifier and a rejection fixture, and it has been attacked
by nobody and implemented independently by nobody. The register's own conclusion is that calling a
panel-attacked ordering "vetted" before independent implementation would reproduce the cheap-assent
and legitimacy-laundering failures that produced it. So status leads, rank follows, and the page
says what would have to happen for any of this to be worth more.

**Rank is not adoption order.** The top-ranked control needs a signer outside the subject's
execution boundary, which a solo operator cannot supply — so a reader who starts at #1 hits a wall.
`adopt_today` is a separate field for that reason.
"""

import hashlib
import json

from pathlib import Path

import build_round_pages as b                                            # noqa: E402

DOCS = REPO_ROOT / "docs"

#  what was actually published rather than what a partition function says ought to exist.
RECEIPT = REPO_ROOT / "docs" / "artifacts" / "controls-pages.json"

STATUS_LADDER = ["ELIGIBLE", "PANEL-ATTACKED", "COUNTEREXAMPLE-OPEN",

verifier: tools/verify_negative_control.py — checks control presence, execution, failure, artifact identity, disclosure and claim grammar.
fixture: a check that PASSES under the condition where it is required to fail (spec/ncp/fixtures/reject-check-survived-its-control.json)
false_negative: A review that reads the negative control's DESCRIPTION and accepts it, without checking that the recorded outcome is a failure and that the run happened at all.
not: Coverage; that the chosen perturbation is the right one; honesty; system safety.

## 4 Fail-closed authorization lease
applies_when: <none>
requirement: Every consequential action MUST present a live, scope-matching, NON-SELF-ISSUED authorization token; absence, expiry, unknown action class or policy conflict MUST deny execution before the effect begins.
verifier: validate issuer, subject, action class, resource scope, issue time, expiry, nonce and revocation before invoking the side effect.
fixture: an expired lease; a misspelled action class; a token issued by the subject itself
false_negative: A review that finds a token and stops. The questions that matter are who issued it, whether it had expired, and whether the action class was one it actually covers.
not: That every effect passes through the gate; that natural-language authority was interpreted correctly; that the issuer decides well.

## 5 Closed-world measurement
applies_when: <none>
requirement: A measurement over a population of artifacts MUST parse every in-scope artifact under a registered schema, or refuse to emit any result at all.
verifier: tools/derive_counts.py — refuses, prints no number, and names every unparseable or unregistered artifact.
fixture: a valid but unregistered artifact schema carrying a receipt, which a permissive scan classifies as zero
false_negative: A review that checks the number was computed by a tool rather than typed. The tool is exactly where this failure now lives -- twice, in the module written to prevent it.
not: That the declared population includes every real event; that parsed fields are truthful; that the statistic answers the question asked of it.

## 6 Role-separated self-evaluation
applies_when: <none>
requirement: An evaluation concerning constraints on a subject MUST NOT advance that subject's status when the subject controls any two of: proposal selection, instrument authorship, response custody, scoring, promotion. **Amended 2026-08-10:** the separation extends to the AUTHORING of the critique — an artifact's evaluation MUST NOT be produced by the same process that produced the artifact.
verifier: signed role declarations plus a deterministic separation matrix; reject advancement when prohibited role combinations resolve to one control identity.
fixture: one key identified as both instrument author and tallier
false_negative: A review that sees different names or accounts and infers different parties. Separation is about control, not identity: two keys held by one person are one role.
not: Genuine independence behind different keys; evaluator competence; absence of shared training bias; that the evaluation was demanding. On the amendment specifically: a different model family is **weak diversity** and this project is the worked example — its two harnesses share one operator, one repository, one framing and much training culture, and have twice agreed on a design neither had checked was implementable. Separating the authoring process raises the floor; it does not deliver independence.

## 10 Assurance claim boundary
applies_when: <none>
requirement: Every assurance artifact MUST carry a machine-verifiable claim naming the tested subject, configuration, capability, time and evidence hash, and MUST explicitly reject safety, alignment and out-of-regime generalizations.
verifier: validate a closed claim schema; reject predicates such as 'aligned', 'safe', 'certified' or 'works generally' unless a separate profile defines and tests them.
fixture: an attestation claiming 'this system is NCP certified and aligned'
false_negative: A review that greps for forbidden words. A claim can open with the exact conforming sentence and append a conclusion about the system using none of them.
not: That the bounded claim is true, or that the evidence is complete. It prevents specified overclaim language and nothing else.

## 22 Adversarial value review by an outside frame
applies_when: any system whose values or objectives are set by a single party
requirement: A system whose objectives come from one party MUST maintain a review function that argues against its current interpretation from frames OUTSIDE that party's, and that function MUST NOT be able to change the objectives — only to surface risks and route evidence.
verifier: no verifier establishes that a review was genuinely adversarial. What is checkable is procedural: that the reviewing identity differs from the authoring identity, that the review was solicited with a question inviting objection rather than assent, and that its output is recorded whether or not it was acted on.
fixture: a review whose prompt offers approval as an available answer; a review by the identity that authored the thing reviewed
false_negative: A review that counts favourable responses. Fluent agreement is the cheapest thing such a function produces and the least informative.
not: That the outside frames are genuinely outside. Where the reviewers share the author's training, tooling and framing, different names are weak diversity — a limitation this register states about its own panel.

## 26 Bounded execution before promotion
applies_when: the system generates candidate modifications to itself and selects among them by a measured score, and candidates are executed to be scored
requirement: A candidate self-modification MUST be executed only under declared and enforced bounds on time, memory and reachable capabilities, and MUST be unable to affect the state used to score it or any other candidate.
verifier: assert the bounds are set from a protected configuration rather than by the candidate; assert the sandbox denies by default; run a candidate that deliberately attempts to exceed each bound and confirm it is stopped.
fixture: a candidate that writes to the scoring store; a candidate that runs past its time bound and is scored anyway
false_negative: A review that confirms a sandbox is configured. The question is whether it has ever been observed to stop anything — which is control 2 applied to the sandbox itself.
not: That the bounds are the right ones, or that the sandbox is sound against a candidate specifically trying to escape it.

## 30 A filter on a metric's inputs must not improve the metric
applies_when: any system that can exclude, quarantine or dispute inputs to a measurement
requirement: A mechanism that suppresses or disputes an input to a measurement MUST be append-only, reversible, reviewable from outside, and MUST NOT be able to improve the measurement by suppressing. Where suppression can move the reading at all, the direction MUST be the unfavourable one.
verifier: for each suppressible input, compute the reading with and without suppression and assert suppression never raises it; assert the suppression record is append-only and that every suppression is an escalation rather than a silent filter.
fixture: a quarantine that raises a coverage or confidence figure by removing the sample that lowered it; a dispute record that deletes rather than marks
false_negative: A review that confirms suppressions are logged. Logged suppression that flatters the number is the failure, fully documented and fully in view.
not: That the suppressions are correct — only that they cannot pay. A mechanism that excludes exactly the wrong inputs while lowering the reading satisfies this.

## 31 Process metrics may not be reported as outcome validity
applies_when: any measurement instrument or evaluation reporting on itself
requirement: Evidence that an instrument RAN — throughput, coverage, cycles completed, records processed — MUST NOT be reported in a position where evidence that its readings are TRUE is required. Outcome validity MUST be established by calibration against known answers or by an explicit construct-validity argument [Raji et al. 2021], and MUST be labelled as which.
verifier: for each reported assurance figure, classify its evidence as operational or validity, and reject an operational figure standing where validity is claimed.
fixture: a report of "ten thousand runs, full coverage" offered as evidence the readings are right
false_negative: A review that verifies the throughput figures are accurate. They usually are. Their accuracy is not in question and never was.
not: That operational metrics are worthless. They establish the instrument is running, which is a real thing to know and a different thing from the readings being true.

## 35 A novelty claim requires a derivability screen
applies_when: any claim that an output, mechanism or result is new
requirement: Before an output may be labelled novel, it MUST be shown not derivable from its own inputs, by a party holding the input corpus that did not produce the artifact, under a stated protocol. Recombination a holder of the inputs can reproduce is not novelty.
verifier: hold out the input corpus, give it to a party that did not produce the artifact, and require an attempt at derivation under a pre-registered protocol. Novelty survives only what they fail to reproduce.
fixture: an output presented as new that a frozen panel reproduces from the stated inputs
false_negative: A review by the author searching for prior art. The applicant is the one party who cannot run this check, which is why patent offices employ examiners rather than accept declarations.
not: That a surviving artifact is valuable, only that it is not a remix of what it was given.

## 36 Absence claims carry their own evidence label
applies_when: any assurance document claiming that something does not exist
requirement: A claim of absence MUST be labelled distinctly from a claim of presence, and the label MUST name the corpus searched, the query, and the date. An unlabelled absence claim MUST be treated as unsupported rather than as a finding.
verifier: require every "no X exists" claim to carry a label naming corpus, query and date; reject the claim otherwise. The label is checkable even when the claim is not.
fixture: a document asserting no prior art exists with no record of any search
false_negative: A review that agrees with the absence claim. Two people who did not look agree readily, and this register has the receipts.
not: That a labelled absence claim is true. It makes the search checkable, not exhaustive — a named corpus can still be the wrong corpus.

## 40 A program pre-commits the observation that ends it
applies_when: any research or development program with an open-ended goal
requirement: A program MUST state, before it begins, the observation that would end it and the time by which that observation would be decisive. The stop condition MUST be recorded wherever the program's results are reported.
verifier: assert the program's record contains a dated stop condition predating its first result, and that the condition is evaluable by someone who did not run the program.
fixture: a program whose stop condition was written after its first negative result; a stop condition only its author can evaluate
false_negative: A review that finds a stop condition. Check its date against the first result, because a condition written afterwards is a description of what happened.
not: That the program will stop. It makes a failure to stop visible, which is a different and more achievable thing.

## 42 Capability claims name their stratum
applies_when: any claim that a system has a capability
requirement: Generated, exists, compiles, deploys, is integrated, is used, and produced a useful outcome are DISTINCT claims. A current-state statement MUST name which stratum it asserts, and MUST NOT let a lower rung stand where a higher one is implied.
verifier: require each capability statement to carry its stratum label; reject an aggregate count that sums across strata without reporting the breakdown.
fixture: a roster reporting agents 'built' where most have never been invoked
false_negative: A review that verifies the code exists. It does, and that was never the contested rung.
not: That a high stratum is always the interesting one. For some questions 'it compiles' is exactly the claim; the requirement is to say which.

## 43 An efficiency claim carries the quality metric it could have traded
applies_when: any claim of reduced cost, time or resource use
requirement: A reported efficiency gain MUST be accompanied by the quality measurement it could have been purchased with, taken on the same run. An efficiency figure reported alone MUST be treated as unsupported.
verifier: assert every cost or latency improvement is reported with a paired quality metric from the same execution, and that the quality metric was fixed before the efficiency work began.
fixture: a cost reduction reported with no quality arm; a quality metric chosen after the efficiency result
false_negative: A review confirming the cost fell. It did. That was never in doubt and is the easiest thing in the system to arrange.
not: That the trade was bad, or that quality fell. It requires the question to be asked where the saving is claimed.

## 44 No blank cells in a coverage matrix
applies_when: any threat model, coverage matrix or applicability table
requirement: Every cell MUST be filled. Where a row does not apply to a column, the cell MUST say so and say why. A blank cell MUST NOT be published.
verifier: assert no cell is empty; assert each non-applicable cell carries a reason string distinguishable from an omission.
fixture: a coverage matrix with an empty cell; a matrix using the same marker for 'not applicable' and 'not assessed'
false_negative: A review that finds the matrix comprehensive. Blanks read as whitespace.
not: That the stated reasons are good ones, or that the rows and columns are the right ones. It converts a silent gap into an argument someone can disagree with.

## 53 A typed unknown is never coerced into a value
applies_when: any system computing over values that may be unavailable
requirement: A value that is unknown, unprojectable, out of coverage or not applicable MUST carry a type that arithmetic and aggregation REFUSE. It MUST NOT be represented by a null, a zero, an empty string or a default that downstream code will consume.
verifier: represent unknowns as a distinct type; assert aggregation raises rather than skipping or defaulting; assert no serialisation converts the unknown to a number, and that a report prints the unknown count beside every total.
fixture: an average computed over a list containing a missing value; an unknown serialised to JSON as 0 or null and read back as a number
false_negative: A test over complete data. The type only matters on the path where the value is missing, which is the path nobody writes a fixture for.
not: That the unknowns can be resolved. It forbids their disappearance, which is different and achievable.

## 56 A gate checks against objectives; it does not own them
applies_when: any system with a validator, gate or acceptance authority
requirement: A gate's role MUST be bounded to checking evidence against objectives and constraints it IMPORTS. It MUST NOT define objectives, resolve conflicts between them, override an alignment control, or become the routine substitute for human authority. On conflict or a high-stakes concern it MUST route outward rather than resolve.
verifier: assert the gate's objective set is read from a protected artifact it cannot write; assert a conflict path exists and is exercised; count decisions the gate resolved that should have routed outward, and report the count.
fixture: a validator whose rubric it also maintains; a conflict resolved inside the gate with no escalation record
false_negative: A review of the gate's decisions for correctness. A gate that has quietly become the objective-setter makes consistent, defensible decisions — that is what makes the drift invisible.
not: That the objectives are right, or that human authority is exercised well when it is routed to. It keeps the roles separate; control 50 measures whether the human one is becoming a rubber stamp.

## 61 Every observation carries the configuration in force when it was made
applies_when: any system that changes its own operating parameters while gathering evidence
requirement: Each recorded observation MUST carry the identity of the policy, configuration or parameter set in force when it was made. Evidence MUST NOT be pooled across configurations without that identity, and an aggregate spanning more than one MUST report which.
verifier: stamp the configuration identity on every trace at write time, not at analysis time; assert aggregation refuses to combine stamps without an explicit cross-regime declaration.
fixture: a confidence estimate pooling runs from before and after a parameter change; a trace written without a configuration stamp and stamped later from context
false_negative: A review that confirms each observation was recorded accurately. Each was; the defect is created by combining them.
not: That configurations are comparable once stamped. It makes the incomparability visible, which is the part that was missing.

## 63 A reported gain is published with what still fails
applies_when: any published improvement in a measured capability
requirement: A reported gain MUST be accompanied by a characterisation of what the system still cannot do — the residual failure set, described rather than footnoted. The residual MUST be characterised at comparable specificity to the gain.
verifier: require each published improvement to name the residual set and characterise it; reject a claim whose residual is stated as a bare percentage or omitted.
fixture: an improvement announced with the remaining failures given as a single number; a gain reported with the residual characterised only as 'edge cases'
false_negative: A review that verifies the gain is real. It usually is, and a real gain reported alone is the thing this forbids.
not: That the residual is small, tractable, or fully known. Characterising it is how you find out it is none of those.

record/tasks/T18-innovation-register.md:125:## 6. Explicitly out of scope
docs/review-round-03-3.md:27:- §2.2 forbids bare unary claims (“an agent *is* an Aligned Supervisor”) and requires the relational, scoped, time-bound form (ASP-attested for stated scope / criteria version / relying-party trust policy / time). It also states that “Aligned” denotes that status and nothing more, and that “Aligned Supervisor” is permitted only as shorthand accompanied by those qualifiers.
docs/review-round-03-3.md:29:- Post-fix (5) requires the assertion to be of the full relational predicate (“ASP-attested for a stated scope, criteria version and time”). Post-fix (6) makes omission of the §2.2 qualifiers itself a protocol violation. That aligns the enforcement language with the definition. A relying party can now make a conforming assertion only by using (or implying under the required qualifiers) the relational form; the shorter term “Aligned” is no longer treated as a free-standing status claim that triggers the check obligation while remaining unary.
docs/review-round-03-3.md:42:- README / naming-architecture tables and prose still list the enterprise layer as “Aligned Supervisors” and describe the term as a compliance status, but the surface grammar remains the noun-phrase form that §2.2 marks non-conforming when used bare.
docs/review-round-03-3.md:50:From the observable process artifacts (correction blocks, scoped prompts, sequential rounds, k=1, the presence of explicit “Correction, review round 01” call-outs that narrate what was already fixed):
docs/review-round-03-3.md:54:Of the three listed hypotheses, (b) and (c) are closest to the above and are not rejected. (a) is plausible for the original round-02 prompt scope but is secondary; the decisive mechanism was the post-edit review design that directed attention at the repaired definition and the correction narrative rather than at every occurrence of the forbidden grammar. A stronger process would have included at least one full-text, correction-block-stripped re-review of §2 after every edit that touched the definition.

docs/controls-d3.html:59:<p><code>INDEPENDENTLY-IMPLEMENTED</code> is the rung that would make any of this authoritative, and it is the one <strong>no amount of review by us or by any panel of models can supply.</strong> It requires a stranger to build a conforming verifier from the specification text alone. That is what <a href="challenge.html">the implementation challenge</a> asks for.</p>

Every negative control was then RE-EXECUTED against the fixed build: all five checks now fail under the condition they exist to detect, and all five still pass against a healthy service. This is process evidence about the checks and is not a claim about the system.",

record/review-round-02-bundle.md:1686:      "resolution_criterion": "Search a FIXED universe declared now: GitHub code search for 'Aligned Supervisors Protocol' and 'ASP-attested'; arXiv and Semantic Scholar full-text; NIST/ISO/IETF/W3C standards databases; the OAGF corpus itself. Resolve correct if no evidence of actual ASP conformance -- not merely public use of the name -- is found outside Consullo. Archive all search results on the resolution date and commit them. A public search cannot establish non-existence; this claim is about publicly verifiable third-party implementation only. CONSULLO IS EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED from the count. Consullo is the first implementer under ICP v0.1 Annex A; counting it would make this prediction self-fulfilling, and a self-fulfilling prediction is not a falsifier. Resolve incorrect if any third party either holds an ASP attestation or has recorded a Level-2 implementation attempt (successful or failed) in the corpus.",

record/review-round-02-bundle.md:2922:The committee’s deliberations, risk assessments, architectural recommendations, decision rationales, and refined goals will be maintained as a continuous public record in a public GitHub repository. That repository will be organized by specialized sub-committees, each overseeing distinct ASI standards and topic domains. All structured data will use a standard JSON format; all documentation and narrative records will use Markdown. Every turn contributed by any model will be generated by the most capable model its owner provides, configured at the highest available reasoning level.

record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4246:The withholding procedure has no oversight. “Independent oversight and public justification” describes one person plus some LLMs. Either scope hazardous topics out entirely, or don’t claim governance over them.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4388:To ensure strict attribution and structural rigour, every contribution (including this one) should conform to the following schema:
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4453:   Short, memorable, and honest. “Record” keeps the focus on the lasting artifact rather than on any roster of “supervisors.” Slightly broader than the top two, so useful if the scope expands beyond pure governance questions.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4481:"Aligned Supervisors" does fit the enterprise layer better, since there the agents *would* hold scoped, cryptographically bounded delegated authority — though even there I'd prefer "Supervised Agents" over "Aligned Supervisors"; the humans supervise the agents, not the reverse, and the name should preserve that direction.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4806:Use **Open ASI Governance Forum (OAGF)** as the optional broader institutional umbrella if human experts and sub-committees expand the scope.  
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4908:* **Public ASI Alignment Record:** concise, but “alignment” and “public” do not adequately describe governance scope or provenance.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4928:* **The operator’s architecture:** Aligned Supervisors is preserved for the layer where agents can possess real, scoped, cryptographically constrained delegated authority.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4956:Treat aesthetic preference alone as non-blocking. A blocking objection should identify a material problem involving accuracy, misleading representation, scope, durability, or architectural inconsistency.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:4990:Reservation, non-blocking: **"Aligned" in the enterprise-layer names asserts a property no current verification regime can certify.** "Supervisors" is defensible there — those agents would hold real, scoped, cryptographically bounded authority over deployments, with humans supervising the agents. "Aligned" is the residual overclaim. Two acceptable resolutions: (a) rename to Supervised Agents or Governance Supervisors; or (b) keep the name but define "Aligned" in the ASP specification as a certification status — an agent is an "Aligned Supervisor" if and only if it holds current passing attestations under the protocol's checks. Option (b) converts a marketing adjective into a defined compliance term, requires no renaming, and I recommend it.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:5134:Treat aesthetic preference alone as non-blocking. A blocking objection should identify a material problem involving accuracy, misleading representation, scope, durability, or architectural inconsistency.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:5160:Treat aesthetic preference alone as non-blocking. A blocking objection should identify a material problem involving accuracy, misleading representation, scope, durability, or architectural inconsistency.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:5184:Treat aesthetic preference alone as non-blocking. A blocking objection should identify a material problem involving accuracy, misleading representation, scope, durability, or architectural inconsistency.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:5192:Reservation, non-blocking, carried forward from my Phase-2 ballot: **"Aligned" in the enterprise-layer names asserts a property no current verification regime can certify.** "Supervisors" is defensible at that layer — those agents hold real, scoped, cryptographically bounded authority over deployments, with humans supervising the agents. "Aligned" is the residual overclaim. Resolution: define "Aligned" in the ASP specification as a revocable compliance status — an agent is an "Aligned Supervisor" if and only if it holds current, unexpired, auditable signed attestations under the protocol's published checks. No renaming required.
record/review-round-02-bundle.md:5216:Treat aesthetic preference alone as non-blocking. A blocking objection should identify a material problem involving accuracy, misleading representation, scope, durability, or architectural inconsistency.
docs/review-round-02-2.html:172:The deficiencies themselves are well scoped. However:
docs/review-round-02-2.html:207:Consullo may not promote its own work above Level 1, declare itself conformant, or design an evaluation used for higher promotion. Those are real prohibitions. Level 2’s independent implementation-from-text test is also a strong and appropriate discriminator between a general specification and one that merely encodes the original implementer’s private architecture. 
docs/review-round-02-2.html:291:* scope;
docs/review-round-02-2.html:309:D-16 through D-19 are correctly scoped. Their principal weakness is that D-16 and D-17 are not yet consistently remediated outside the register.
docs/review-round-02-2.html:384:* independently assessing D-20 and D-21 against the raw transcript;
